# Progress

Status of every roadmap milestone (design.md §20) and orchestrator work package (`docs/prompts/initial-orchestrator.md`).
Statuses: **todo** / **in progress** / **done** / **blocked: needs playtest** / **blocked: other** (with reason).

"Done" means: `./gradlew build` passes, `./gradlew :neoforge:runGameTestServer` passes, and the new logic has tests.

Last updated: 2026-10-07 (fifth session, continued after a usage-limit cut: GR2, MAP2, RS2, W0b, GL1, CT1, ST1, HELM1, CR1, RP1 merged; MAP3, GR3, ST2, ART1a verified and waiting for the stage; WG1, HM1, CW1, FL2, ST3, Q5, ART1b cut off mid-work, see the paused note under phase P).

## Phase F: first playtest feedback (done, F5 and F7 continue in phase G)

The human played milestones 1 to 3 and reported: the dry hull works well; a floating ship rocks from side to side without end; slabs, stairs and trapdoors show water in their empty half; the blocks look like 2012 and need real 3D models; sails should be built from two yards with cloth between them, and triangular sails from a rope and a cleat; flags are far too small; the anchor should be a real anchor on the hull side with chain sound and a splash; planks should creak now and then; the captain's whistle should open a radial menu. These are recorded as decisions in design.md §4.3, §4.7, §4.8, §5.2, §5.3, §7.2, §16 and §21.

| Package | Scope | Status |
|---|---|---|
| F1 | Roll and pitch damping (the rocking was undamped roll) and occasional hull creaking tied to the rolling, with placeholder vanilla sounds | done | Merged. `HullDampingModel` (defaults 1.5/1.5; a kicked 7×17 hull settles in 3 s instead of rocking for 8), `audio/` module with the creak trigger and the generated `sounds.json`. The endless rolling could not be reproduced headlessly: Sable damps small hulls in the test basin itself, so the in-game fix needs the playtest (`milestone-3.md` §5c). |
| F2 | Occlusion regions include watertight partial blocks (slabs, stairs, trapdoors) on the hull's inside | done | Merged. A partial cell joins the dry region when every uncovered face looks at a dry cell or a block, and never when one looks at the sea or the sky. Toggle `dry_hull.partial_blocks`. Playtest `milestone-2.md` §11c. |
| F3 | The captain's whistle opens a radial menu; orders go to the server as a payload | done | Merged. Four entries (hoist, reef, furl, release crew), click or hold-aim-release, Escape closes. The sneak-use cycling is gone. The screen itself is unverified: playtest `milestone-4.md` §9. |

Playtest `milestone-3.md` part 2 §4 and §5. |

Both sides textured, 32×16 textures. Playtest `flags.md`. |

Phase F left `main` green (627 JUnit tests, 190 GameTests, four green runs). Milestone 0 was playtested by the human and passed completely.

Follow-ups from phase F (small):
- ~~`sounds.json` has one owner~~ done in G1: `data.sounds(...)` collects entries from all modules, and the anchor plays its own events.
- `SailingRuntime` applies the anchor force at the capstan centre; it should act at the hawse (`ShipAnchor.hawse`), which is now 2 to 3 blocks to the side. Harmless within the anchor's slack.
- `StationGameTests.crewHoistsAndFurlsAfterTheWorkTime` failed once for two different agents ("sails not furled: FULL") in about 12 runs each, and passed in all of the orchestrator's 20 runs. Its ±3 tick margin may be too tight under load.
- ~~Custom banner flags show a generic cloth~~ done in G8 (base colour only; banner patterns are not shown).

Decided (2026-10-07, the human connected Blockbench): Blockbench models are hand-made assets. `.bbmodel` sources in `art/models/`, exports in `common/src/main/resources/assets/pirates_n_ships/models/`, renders in `art/renders/`, datagen writes only block states and item models. Written into `CLAUDE.md` and design.md §4.8.

## Phase G: sound, multi-block sails and 3D models (running)

Started 2026-10-07 after the session restart with the Blockbench MCP connected (the MCP tools reach subagents; checked). The human added eight Pixabay files to `raw_sound/` (git-ignored): four music tracks (Leave Her Johnny; The Ghost Of Gallows Reef; There Be Pirates - The Quest; There Be Pirates - Lost in the Deep) and four gun sounds (pistol shot, empty gun shot, cannon shot, artillery gunfire), and will add more over time. Sources are listed in `docs/credits.md` once G1 lands.

## Phase P: second playtest feedback (running)

The human played with the phase G build on 2026-10-07 and reported: figureheads face the same way as the player instead of looking at the player with the plate on the clicked block; the brig door still has the old item sprite and does not connect with the brig bars, and a key should lock and unlock it; pistol and musket show the bow pose while holding right click but should aim (and whether reloading works at all: it does, hold right click with lead shot and gunpowder); the doubloon and the bounty proof should be 3D too; cannons need sizes: a large cannon whose carriage takes two blocks in length and 1.5 in height with the barrel one to two blocks ahead, plus a small swivel gun that mounts on a railing, turns around, and has a shorter range (no 1×1×1 cannon); the sail direction is detected exactly 180° wrong.

| Package | Scope | Status |
|---|---|---|
| P1 | Code fixes: figureheads mount on the clicked block facing the player; the cloth bellies downwind (convention fix); brig bars connect to the door and a brig key locks and unlocks it; firearms load with the crossbow pose and a loaded gun aims while held and fires on release, with an aimed spread bonus | done | Merged (`2f1f0d5`). Figureheads: `FACING` = the clicked face. Sails: **no inversion found**, client and server share the flow-vector convention (`ClothSide` extracted, 6 JUnit tests incl. the spec case); the likely cause of the report is the flag, whose wind facing ignores the ship's orientation (fixed in P4). Brig: bars connect to door halves; `brig_key` (ingot over nugget) toggles the lock, any key on any door, the owner still opens a locked door, sneak-use without a key does nothing. Firearms: hold sessions read from the remaining use time (loading 36000 ticks, aiming 72000), release fires, `firearms.aim` (`aim_min_ticks` 0, `aim_steady_ticks` 20, `aimed_spread_factor` 0.5), client `firearm_view.musket_zoom` 1.25 through a new `ClientEvents.COMPUTE_FOV`; **no pose** during loading or aiming because vanilla's crossbow poses are hard-wired to `CrossbowItem` (P3 adds poses through PAL). 10 JUnit and 6 GameTests. |
| P4 | Flag wind direction in the ship's frame: `FlagpoleBlockEntity#windFacing` must turn the world wind into plot coordinates by the ship's orientation, re-evaluate when the ship turns, and sample the wind at the ship's world position | done | Merged (`828c2c4`). `FlagWind.shipFrame`/`downwindOnShip` (orientation inverse, vertical part dropped), the wind sampled at the pole's world position, re-checked every `flags.ship_update_interval_ticks` (20) on ships, 200 on land. 7 JUnit and 1 GameTest (ship turned 180° and 90°, land flag untouched). The first run exposed a test leaking a wind override into another batch; fixed in the new test. |
| FL1 | Flags stream at the exact wind angle (third playtest) | done | Merged (`1cbbb04`). The cloth left the block model: `FlagClothRenderer` (block entity renderer, `entityCutoutNoCull`, the banner base colour as vertex tint, a 1.6-block render box) draws `FlagClothModel`'s plain geometry at a continuous yaw; `FlagpoleBlockEntity` stores and syncs two bearings (`windBearing`, the world direction the wind blows toward, and `yaw` in the drawing frame) on the existing intervals, syncing only on a change over 0.25° without a block-state change; on a ship the client turns the wind bearing into the ship's render orientation every frame, so the cloth stays downwind between server checks; `FlagWind.downwindAngle` (pure; calm or a wind without a horizontal part keeps the last angle), `FlagYaw.approach` eases the drawn angle the short way with a 4-tick time constant and jumps on the first frame. The block state shows only the pole in every state; `FACING` is unused (kept as the starting angle of old poles). 24 JUnit tests, the P4 GameTest extended with 45° and a wind change. |
| P5 | Firearm usability from the third playtest: sneak lowers an aimed gun without firing, a loading progress bar on the item, the loaded state visible in the inventory | done | Merged (`9059d43`). `FirearmRules.lowers`/`aimsOnUse` (a release while sneaking in an aim session never fires; a loaded gun is not raised while sneaking, else vanilla's repeated use would flicker it), `firearms.aim.lower_on_sneak` (true), client `FirearmLowering` releases the use at `CLIENT_TICK_END` when sneaking (after that tick's sneak command, so the server sees the sneak first; vanilla's own release would go first in the same tick). `FirearmBar` (white 0..13 px over the reload, gold full when loaded, nothing when empty) through `isBarVisible/getBarWidth/getBarColor` with a `FirearmClientState` supplier (no `Minecraft` in the item). | Q3 | Cause of the one-off `CrewPoseGameTests.workingFlagFollowsTheOrder` failure | done | Merged (`843d2a6`). **Cause:** two tests in one batch overrode the same config values, and the framework starts a batch's tests a few ticks apart (chunk loading is asynchronous), so the earlier test's restore wrote the outer value (`TICKS_PER_TRIM_STEP` 40) back while the later test's order started, making its hoist 80 ticks; the same flaw leaked `TICKS_PER_TRIM_STEP = 10` and `INFLOW_RATE = 10` from earlier batches into the rest of the suite. Reproduced deterministically by shifting one test by a tick. **Fix:** `ConfigOverrides.Handle.restore` hands its original value to the newest active override of the same value, so overrides restore correctly in any order (3 JUnit tests); five batches split so no batch holds two overrides of one value (crew pose, 12 station winch tests, creak, damping, dry-hull inflow); a scan that follows helper methods finds no remaining case. Production code and the margins were right (worst-case tick arithmetic in the test comments). |
| Q4 | Cause of the intermittent `SharkGameTests.sharkStaysInWaterAndCruises` failure | done | Merged (`e7d14f2`). **A real bug:** `SmoothSwimmingMoveControl` with `applyGravity = true` adds +0.005 y velocity per tick in water and vanilla's dolphin cancels it in `travel`; the shark's copied `travel` did not, so idle sharks drifted up and breached for a tick or two (3 of 40 instrumented runs; most cruised right at the surface, which is 8/9 of a block high, not the block top). Also `getRandomSwimmablePos` can return an air target. **Fix:** neutral buoyancy (`applyGravity = false`), `Shark.travel` clamps upward speed so the box bottom stays `SURFACE_MARGIN` 0.25 under the water surface (covers the lunge), the cruise goal rejects non-water targets; the test also fails on any rise above the margin, plus a new test forcing upward speed. 0 breaches in 140 instrumented runs (100 under load). |
| M5 | The pirate struggles to approach a player and never lands a hit (third playtest) | done | Merged (`da9aad1`). **Causes** (measured with scripted moving players): the pirate's ground speed (0.198 blocks/tick) was below a walking player's (0.216); attacks started at the edge of reach against the target's current position, so a 5-tick wind-up missed anyone stepping back; ground navigation refuses to search while airborne (just spawned, knocked back), leaving the mob standing for ~19 ticks; and the target scan ignored `canAttack` (on peaceful the target would flap). **Fix:** pure `mob/ai/ChaseRules` (gap to the target's footprint, smoothed closing speed, attack only when the gap predicted for the first hit frame is inside reach − `duelist_reach_margin`, stop at reach − `duelist_approach_margin` with hysteresis, vanilla-like path cadence 4–10 ticks, at most 5 searches/s), `DuelistAttackGoal` walks at `mobs.duelist_chase_speed` 1.15 (faster than walking, slower than sprinting) and keeps walking during its own wind-up and hit frames; `HostileTargetGoal` checks `canAttack`; `DuelistBrain.explain` names the rule behind each decision; `/pirates mob debug on|off` traces target, duelist, brain and path changes of mobs within 24 blocks to the log and chat (prints the difficulty). Kiting player: first hit at tick 15–18 instead of 178. 6 JUnit and 5 GameTests. |
| M6 | The navy soldier's musket aim and reload animations (third playtest: reload not shown, aim looks idle) | done | Merged (`255b50b`). **Finding:** the procedural aim pose did run (GeckoLib applies `setCustomAnimations` after the controllers), but it lasts only the 20 aim ticks per shot, the 100-tick reload had no pose, and under 4 blocks the soldier backs off and never aims; the old pose also left the musket pointing up. **Built:** `musket_aim` (0.35 s hold), `musket_reload` (5 s once, stretched to `musket_reload_ticks` by a speed handler) and `musket_shove` (0.5 s) added to the shared `crew_member.animation.json` (the four crew animations byte-identical), poses mapped from the player's P3/F8h firearm poses with `right_hand` carrying the gun; synced `DATA_MUSKET` (action + reload length) from `NavySoldier.customServerAiStep`, two GeckoLib controllers on top of the body controller (aim/reload with blend, shove layered), the look pitch and head yaw added to both arms while aiming and the head tipped 6° in code; soldiers are never left-handed. Source `art/models/entity/musket_animations.js`, renders `anim_mob_musket_*.png`. 7 JUnit (+ rig checks) and 1 GameTest. Duelist telegraphs unchanged. |
| M6b | The soldier's musket pointed the wrong way during the reload (third playtest) | done | Merged (`8ac248e`). **Cause:** GeckoLib 4.9.3 rotates a held item by its hand bone twice (`GeoEntityRenderer.renderRecursively` applies the bone, then `BlockAndItemGeoLayer.renderForBone` applies it again through `translateAndRotateMatrixForBone`), so every `right_hand` key turned the musket double: the reload lay level at the hip pointing back and right, the aim pointed 73° down and 66° right by the numbers. **Fix:** `HumanoidGeoRenderer.HeldItemLayer` overrides `renderForBone` and only moves to the bone's pivot (`ItemFrame.apply`) before vanilla's x −90 and the item transform. Animations unchanged (byte-identical); GeckoLib's bone translation and PAL's item translation turned out to be the same move. Three `SeafarerRigTest` tests compose the drawn musket through the real GeckoLib, vanilla and PAL transforms (aim level and forward, reload muzzle within 20° of up with the butt on the ground, shove butt-first); restoring the double rotation fails them. The aim changes on screen too: the playtest must confirm it. |

## Phase M: NPCs and animations (running)

Started 2026-10-07 on the human's request ("what about all the NPCs and the animations? can't you already start doing that?"). GeckoLib was not in the build yet and the crew member used the vanilla humanoid model, so the NPC work starts with a GeckoLib spike that also fixes the rig contract the mob models follow; the sword animations are authored in Blockbench in parallel.

**State for the next session (written 2026-10-07 at the end of the fourth session):** `main` is green (831 JUnit tests, 257 GameTests, three green runs after every merge), nothing is running, no agent branch or worktree is left, 150 commits since the start of the session. Phase G is complete: every package from G1 to G13 and every Blockbench batch (F7a to F7f, F8a to F8e) is merged; every block and held item has a Blockbench model except the doubloon, the bounty proof, the hull patch and the brig door item. Milestones 5 to 10 and 13 to 15 all gained real pieces; what each still lacks is in the roadmap table. **The human's playtest backlog is the gate now:** milestones 1 to 4 with the phase F and G changes, then the phase G checklists (`firearms.md`, `melee.md`, `pump-and-patch.md`, `cannons.md`, `grapple.md`, `market-desk.md`, `audio.md`), plus `items-and-blocks.md` for every model and `flags.md` for banners and nameplates. Open design questions: stability and the sail force scale (§21, milestone 3 playtest), the sword's right click taking over doors and the helm (melee.md), hauling feel (grapple.md). Work that needs no playtest and no Blockbench: crew hiring, wages and morale (15), navy NPCs and notice boards (13), ports with positions once the human's structures exist (11), the follow-ups listed above. Blockbench work still open: Blockbench-authored sword animations (`art/animations/`, replacing the G12 placeholders), the flag cloth at 2 blocks (block entity renderer), the brig door item, entity models for the mobs (12, needs a GeckoLib integration spike like G12 first).

**Incident (F5b merge): stale GameTest config.** The F5b branch passed its GameTests in the agent's worktree but the beam-reach keel test failed deterministically on the merged tree (leeway 0.139 instead of 0.111 m/s). Cause: the GameTest server keeps the mod's config in `neoforge/build/gametest/config/`, which the fresh-world task did not clear, and the orchestrator's copy still had `keel_lateral_drag = 2.0` from an old default (the current default is 8.0), while every fresh agent worktree ran with 8.0. So orchestrator and agent measurements have differed for a while, which explains part of the earlier "flaky" sailing tests. Fixed in `neoforge/build.gradle`: `runGameTestServer` now also deletes `build/gametest/config/pirates_n_ships-*.toml`.

Follow-ups from phase G (small):
- **Usage limit incident (2026-10-07, fifth session; the session continued after the human changed account, WG1 and FL2 were resumed from their transcripts, the rest kept running):** the state at the cut was: landed on `main` this session: GR2, MAP2, RS2, W0b, GL1, CT1, ST1, HELM1 (+HELM1b), CR1, RP1. **Verified by their agents and waiting for the merge stage** (branches under `.claude/worktrees/agent-*`): MAP3 (`worktree-agent-a3f4f47fedd757d53`; its stage was running in `.claude/worktrees/merge` when the session ended and lands by itself if it finishes, without its docs row), GR3 (`worktree-agent-a37e562a5844104d9`), ST2 (`worktree-agent-aea283f88410c907e`), ART1a (`worktree-agent-a3b592360afaef51f`; ART1b's branch already contains it). **Cut off mid-work, partial work in their worktrees:** WG1 (world module placing the village, port registry), HM1 (hammock and morale), CW1 (cargo weight on ships), FL2 (flags in the world), ST3 (navy outpost pieces, lab port 8768), Q5 (the human's "no sails / 0 of 0 crew" bug), ART1b (flag and map tile items, Blockbench). Resume each with `git merge main` first; the stage procedure and its rules are in the orchestrator's memory notes (merge in `.claude/worktrees/merge`, runData, `tools/gen_guideme.py`, build, GameTests, ff or docs-only merge, regenerate the guide after editing `docs/guide.md`). The human's dev client runs from the main checkout: never build there.
- **Paused by the weekly model limit (2026-10-07), resumed in the fifth session (phase S):** four packages were cut off mid-work and keep their partial, uncommitted work in their worktrees under `.claude/worktrees/`: CR1 (orders instead of assignments: a per-ship job board where free crew take the work; just started), GR2 (slide along a taut grapple rope: unit tests passed, datagen and GameTests pending), RS2 (the Shipwright's Toolkit and the two-click rejoin of split pieces: rules test in progress), MAP2 (the map tile block drawn from a chart region: datagen pending). Resume them (same prompts, `git merge main` first) once the limit resets at 17:00 Berlin time. Also pending: the human's new `art/schematics/starter_sloop_basic.schem` (682 blocks, same size and helm position as the committed sloop) needs converting and registering as a replacement or a second template (W0b).
- Splits (RS1): pairing Sable's announced groups with the next allocated sub-level relies on allocation order (guarded by an anchor check); `assembly.split.enabled = false` switches Sable's splitting off for every sub-level on the server; the wreck persist timer resets on restart and removes without sinking (TODO); relocation memory lasts 6000 ticks; the anchor on a keeper whose identity moved is dropped by the sailing scan; "Wreck of" is translatable, the Sable name is English.
- Chart (MAP1): up to 1 MB per online player in memory at the default cap; about 2,400 column reads per player every 40 ticks (unprofiled with many players); a first open at the widest zoom streams up to about 300 regions over a few seconds; at most 64 region textures cached on the client (about 16 MB worst case, not seen in game); changing `cell_blocks` clears explored cells; dropped regions stay in the client cache until logout; the item sprite is flat (Blockbench later); the key category lang key `key.categories.pirates_n_ships` must be reused by the next key mapping; ship blocks (sub-levels) do not show on the chart.
- Grapple (GR1): the weapon belongs in the off hand (a musket in the main hand gets the use first: a click then the shot, or it starts loading with ammo); tied hooks do not survive logout (hooks are not persisted); a tied owner dying in another dimension leaves the hook where it hangs; the client skips its release request when aiming at a ring based on the same plot-coordinate assumption as the server (sable-notes §9.0e); rings on land do not catch hooks (G11 treats land as a miss) but can take a tied near end; the ring has a placeholder button model (Blockbench later).
- Flags (FL1): a wind change reaches the cloth up to one interval late, then eases in; a turning ship syncs each flag about once a second (unmeasured bandwidth with many flags); the cloth is lit from the pole's block; `FlagpoleBlock`'s javadoc still describes `FACING` and the cloth block model (the property is unused since FL1 and could be dropped at the cost of old poles' starting angle); a 2-block cloth is possible now with new textures from `tools/gen_flag_textures.py`, whose docstring still mentions the block model.
- Ship templates (W0): the starter sloop's waterline row 2 is a guess (one number in `ShipTemplates.STARTER_SLOOP`, then `runData`); the water surface is searched only from the caller's feet + 4 down to − 48 (sea level fallback with a warning); `force` overwrites without drops; the cleat's and the capstan's `rotate()` are unchecked for the turned placements; future schematics: bow north or a `bow` field, exactly one helm, everything connected, under the block limit, a tight selection, no terrain, and the intended waterline row.
- Kraken (K1b/K1c): the fins ride on the mantle's scale (a `fin_left`/`fin_right` bone under `mantle` would need the rig contract and `KrakenRigTest` changed); the beak is under water (move it below the eyes if it should show from a deck); an arm pointing about 24.5° inward off vertical (through the head or under the body) can roll fast (singular sucker reference since GL1); at the 10-block reach arms stretch 2.7× at unchanged width; 533 cubes per kraken; mantle or position keys shift the head against the tentacle roots by 1–2 px during surface and submerge; the mantle reaches above the culling box; body texel density (0.68) is lower than the tentacles' (1).
- GUI (U1): parchment speckle and ink readability, and the alpha of the fade reaching sprite and fill shaders, are unchecked in game; the survival bar position assumes vanilla's HUD rows (other mods' bars may overlap; `y_offset` fixes it); the market's coin is the kit sprite, not the doubloon item.
- Hats (H2): the head transform is derived from the sources and the geo, not seen in game (y is 1.6 json units per px; a cockade on the wearer's right means x is mirrored); big outer-layer hair may show through the tricorn walls by about 0.5 px; palette colours are close to the mob skins, not identical; navy hats as a disguise for the false-flag logic (§13) is open.
- Models (V1/V1b): edit a gun's base project only with `lint_models.py --fix --mirror` afterwards so the loaded variant stays in step; `tools/gen_cannon_models.py` regenerates the old geometry (run the lint fix after it); 693 hidden face pairs remain (quads wasted, no flicker); a 0.05 px step can leave a hairline gap at grazing angles (watch the water barrel boards); palette patches are assumed uniform.
- Guide (D1): item links only inside tables (not prose), sub-sections are headings rather than sub-pages, English only, no images or scenes; `<ItemLink>` in GFM table cells and `\<` escapes are confirmed from GuideME's source only; a `<Row>` of 20+ recipes may be tall; a client without GuideME joining a server with it hits GuideME's own registry mismatch.
- Kraken (K1a): real players' swipe and drag movement (`hurtMarked`) is unverified in game (the tentacle aim and sucker roll are proven by `KrakenWorldPoseTest` since GL1); `plotBlocks()` is scanned every 10 ticks while attacking (watch very large ships); the separation check sees only entity-loaded chunks; its own movement can bump the hull; persistence across a reload and the no-player despawn are untested. A mast strike (or a cannonball through a mast) lets Sable split the cut-off rig into its own body (see the reconnect proposal).
- Playtest finding (mobs): "no damage from any mob attacks" is vanilla's peaceful rule (`Player.hurt` zeroes difficulty-scaled damage); the playtest files now say to use normal difficulty. If it recurs on normal, investigate the damage path for real `ServerPlayer`s (the GameTests use mock `Player`s).
- Mobs (M6b): any other GeckoLib `BlockAndItemGeoLayer` with animated hand bones would rotate items twice (ours overrides `renderForBone`; re-check `SeafarerRigTest` after a GeckoLib update); the rig tests sample key times, not eased frames. Mobs (M6): a soldier first seen mid-reload restarts the reload animation; the shove pose is approximate; the open `navy_soldier` Blockbench tab holds older crew animations than the shipped file (the source is `crew_member.bbmodel` plus the scripts).
- Hazards (H1): waterspouts are stationary (only whirlpools drift); lifted entities hover where lift equals gravity instead of being thrown out of the top; each affected player gets one push payload per tick; boats dragged under rely on vanilla's 60-tick eject; particles from 96 blocks may look faint; the spawner GameTest loads a chunk outside the test area; `SettingsConfigTest.ownValuesAreWellFormed` bound lowered to 44 because the six hazards values left the settings sections.
- Law (L1): with an empty main hand and no prisoner in range the click passes to the off hand, so a proof there is turned in on the same click; board sessions hold a `ServerPlayer` reference until the next refresh or logout; `delivery_range` is measured prisoner-to-officer; known names come from the board and online players only (no profile cache, which could block on Mojang). 
- Melee sounds (P8/A2): the unused first whoosh of the swipes file could become a seventh variant; `clash6` is about 6 dB quieter than the other clangs (drop it if weak); blade on blade beats armour on a mid-attack armoured defender; the Sword Fight page URLs in the manifest are inferred from the slug pattern; a slash into a crowd plays one hit per target; switching between two different swords after the cooldown sounds each time; a failed config test could leave a forwarding recording sink installed for the rest of a run.
- Sea chest: no paddling or riding yet (use opens the chest, so vanilla boarding is not free); the floating chest and Sable hulls only collide (a chest drifting into a hull is untested); the sink pull and sprint block act in the client player tick (confirm on a dedicated server); the worn tooltip shows a hint line instead of vanilla's "When on Body" modifiers; `SeaChestTestSupport.playerInLevel` duplicates `MobTestSupport` (candidate for `core/gametest`).
- Mobs (M5): the chase speed is a balance choice; attack timing predicts the gap and still misses a player who changes direction during the wind-up (normal attack pause follows); the debug command's lines are plain English (lang keys would need `MobModule`'s datagen); `DuelistDebug` listeners are static (call `clear()` from a server-stopped hook); on peaceful difficulty pirates ignore players like vanilla mobs.
- Survival: the Fabric port needs a forwarder for `ITEM_USE_FINISH` (no Fabric event; a small mixin on `LivingEntity.completeUsingItem`); the swim cost is charged per tick from position deltas (vanilla charges per movement packet, rounding may differ slightly); the cold-water hook loops over the level's entities every tick (cheap checks first).
- Shark (M4-art): mirror faces with negative u sizes are not covered by a test (check both sides look the same in game); the bite's head position offset may show a seam at the back of the head. Shark (Q4): the 0.25 surface margin is untuned (if sharks hunt surface swimmers too deep, lower it or raise the bite reach); currents and pushes can still lift a shark out (intended); without buoyancy a shark whose path ended keeps its depth until the next cruise target. Shark: weight and group config take effect at the next server start (the biome modifier reads them once); the root pitch sign, jaw and fin directions are unverified in game; circling and lunging are tuned for open water (near walls, kelp or a moving hull it may scrape or bump); spawned sharks despawn like fish unless named; the cod drop is never cooked.
- Cannons (F7g): the swivel item looks dark in the GUI (`anvil` texture, consistent with the cannon); the first-person display entries of both items were not checked visually.
- Cannons (P2): the swivel's aim relies on vanilla repeating block uses while right-click is held plus our `INTERACTION_KEY` cancel (unverified in game); a lost release payload keeps the gun manned until the player walks away; the two-block model may be clipped by section culling at some angles; `swivel_mounts` names the brig bars optionally and there is no dedicated railing block; the cannon item still shows the one-block hand-made model until F7g; `CannonStation`/`CannonService` resolve the master redundantly now that `Stations.at` does.
- Mobs: players see an NPC's feint recovery as a normal recovery (no player feint input or animation yet; `MeleeService.feint` is the entry point); a feint against a guard aborts at wind-up tick 2, so weapons with a 2-tick wind-up never feint; strolling mobs can walk off a small deck (guards use the `stationary` flag); spawn egg colours and the telegraph arm rotation signs are unverified in game; navy target checks call the law service every tick while targeting; `removeWhenFarAway` is false so mobs never despawn. `tools/gen_entity_textures.py` took M3's version at the merge, so the M2 sailor-skin generator code is gone from it (the committed `crew_member.png` and the `.bbmodel` keep the skin; M2's rebuild scripts are in `art/models/entity/`); re-add the crew generator when the mob skins get their Blockbench pass.
- Firearms: holding to aim slows walking like a bow; releasing right-click in the same tick as the sneak press still fires (vanilla's release packet goes first); crouch-firing is impossible with `lower_on_sneak` on (switch to an edge-triggered lower payload if wanted); the creative inventory's stack copies may not show the white loading bar; the cocked-hammer variants came in P6; the Fabric port needs the access-widener line for `ItemProperties.register`. Brig: `message...brig.door.not_owner` and `DoorLockRules.canChangeLock` are unused since the key. The brig door item shows the unlocked door without a padlock; its third-person pose was tuned in Blockbench's preview only (may clip the leg while walking).
- Crew rig: a crew member standing on a moving deck may play `walk` if the vanilla limb swing picks up the deck motion; `sit` never plays at stations (the seat makes it stand); `CLAUDE.md` now lists GeckoLib and PAL as hard dependencies.
- Sword animations: a crouched attack likely pops the upper body to standing height (the position channels replace vanilla's sneak offsets; fix by skipping the lean and twist compensation while sneaking, in Java, or additive positions); a stagger interrupting a pose and a slash riposte snap one frame; the rest pose ignores the walk arm swing (small jump while walking); first-person framing of the wide slash is unchecked.
- ~~Plunder crime on the direct sell command~~ done in Q1. Water barrel: the item model still shows `blue_ice` water (an item colour hook `ClientEvents.registerItemColor` would let it use the tint); the water-barrel `.bbmodel` projects do not carry the `tintindex` and `water_still` edits made to the exported models. `station/order` imports `combat.cannon.CannonStation.CannonOrder`, a station-combat package cycle that an order registry would remove.
- Grapple: hauling strength, closing speed (up to about 3 m/s bumps) and yaw are untuned; the rope damping still uses Sable's reported velocities, which are unreliable at contact (it only reduces the pull); the nearest-block search scans the thrower's ship every 10 ticks; hooks are not saved with the world; `CombatContent`'s class comment is stale about the hook and the firearms.
- Animations: rotation signs and the `right_item` orientation of the placeholders are unverified; a parry from idle snaps to the guard pose (no fades, PAL's fade API can't start mid-animation); `layer_priority` applies after a restart; `common` depends on PAL's NeoForge jar bundling the Common and Core classes (a later PAL could stop doing that, which would break the build loudly).
- The brig door's item sprite (`textures/item/brig_door.png`) still shows a wooden door and no longer matches the iron cell door; redraw it or use the 3D model. The bilge pump's handle reaches above its block and its spout past the front edge (visual overlap with neighbours).
- ~~Market: `MarketBackend.onNoticedPlunder` has no subscriber~~ done in G13 (desk and opened-market sales; the direct sell command is the remaining gap). ~~Stale prices for a second player and no close payload~~ done in T1 (sessions refresh once a second while prices recover, which is steady traffic on a busy server; command-opened markets are refreshed too and end only on logout, death or another market; the direct sell command relies on the tick refresh). Ports need positions and display names (world package) so desks bind on placement.
- Cannons: hitting another ship's crew is not yet a crime against that ship; claims are not respected (no vanilla API); a bounced ball may stick at the face on the client until the next entity update; the bounce ignores the ship's own motion at the hit point; spawn protection judges a ship block by block at its world position. Crew supply (C9): the latest order wins, so "Load!" inside the 10-tick fuse of a "Fire!" interrupts the shot (station-wide semantics); the supply scan is (2r+1)³ lookups per order (35k at the max range 16); a two-block cannon measures the range from its master block; containers are read as plain `Container` without sided rules or locks.
- Crew at a pump stop when the bilge is dry and need a new order when water returns; an "auto-pump while manned" option would fix that. A crew member seated in a flooding hold may drown in a long flood (check in the playtest).
- Melee input takes the right mouse button completely while a mod sword is held: no doors, chests, levers, helm or station use with a sword in hand. Decide after the playtest: pass USE through when the crosshair targets an interactable block or entity, or when sneaking. Also: attacking out of a held guard drops the guard until right click is pressed again; late trackers learn a fighter's state at its next change; the Fabric port needs a small mixin on `Minecraft.startAttack/startUseItem/continueAttack` for the interaction hook.
- Pump: the model is a datagen placeholder for a Blockbench batch; placing a patch while the ship moves is unverified (playtest). The whistle and command order come with G7.
- Firearms: whether lead balls collide with Sable ship blocks, and whether a server player's inherited deck velocity is current when firing from a moving ship, are unverified (playtest `firearms.md`). The musket reuses the pistol shot sound at a lower pitch until a musket recording exists. `CombatContent`'s class Javadoc still says firearms have no behaviour.
- The rapier's item-frame (`fixed`) entry is at vanilla scale, so its tip sticks about 2 px out of the frame; very thin faces (0.5 px tip) may flicker at distance. Decide after the playtest.
- `.gitattributes` marks `*.gradle` as CRLF (template leftover), so Git warns about `neoforge/build.gradle`; switch it to LF when convenient.
- Stays and mirrored structure templates: the cleat facing is mirrored but the stored stay offset is not, so the stay is ignored after a mirrored placement. Matters once ship templates exist.
- GameTest rule (Q3): one config override per value per batch; `ConfigOverrides` now tolerates any restore order, but a test that reads a value another test in the same batch overrides still races. Earlier "flaky" reports may have been leaked `INFLOW_RATE`/`TICKS_PER_TRIM_STEP` values.
- GameTest rule (Q3): one config override per value per batch; `ConfigOverrides` now tolerates any restore order, but a test that reads a value another test in the same batch overrides still races. Earlier "flaky" reports may have been leaked `INFLOW_RATE`/`TICKS_PER_TRIM_STEP` values.
- The beam-reach GameTests run close to their bounds (0.371 m/s against 0.3). Physics has been deterministic so far; watch them.
- The yard cloth is a flat grid with a simple belly and one light value; the Fabric port needs a culling-box equivalent (`shouldRenderOffScreen` breaks Sable's sub-level path, see sable-notes §9.0g). F7 may give the cloth a better look.
- The yard model shows its rope band on every block of a yard (a model can't know which block meets the mast); the capstan bars and the winch crank reach 3 px into neighbouring blocks. Visual only; revisit after the playtest.
- The rope on the winch and yard is vanilla stripped birch as a stand-in for a rope texture.
- Saving a `.bbmodel` from Blockbench embeds the vanilla textures again; strip them before committing (F7a did it with an ad-hoc script; a `tools/strip_bbmodel_textures.py` would make it one command).
- The creak variants came out 8 LU below the −16 LUFS target (peak-limited); if they are too faint next to the waves and music, raise `hull_creaking` `maxVolume`, use two-pass loudnorm or a lower true-peak target. The gun effects came out 2 to 3 LU below the −16 LUFS target (single-pass `loudnorm` with the true-peak limit). Raise `EFFECT_LUFS` or use two-pass if they sound quiet next to vanilla.
- Fabric port: `SELECT_MUSIC` and `SOUND_STREAM_STARTED` have no direct Fabric equivalent and may need a mixin there.
- `.gitattributes` had `* text eol=lf`, which would have corrupted the `.ogg` files; `*.ogg/.mp3/.wav binary` (G1) and `*.nbt binary` (orchestrator) were added. Existing PNGs were already covered.


## Phase S: fifth session (running)

Started 2026-10-07 after the weekly model limit reset. The previous orchestrator's context was lost with its session, so the four paused packages were resumed from their worktrees with reconstructed prompts (the agents' own javadocs and config comments carried the design). The human also asked for two new things: ship structures built with the `minecraft-schematic-lab` MCP (`.mcp.json`; it was disabled in `.claude/settings.local.json`, the orchestrator may not edit that file, so the human enables it), and steering by turning the wheel with input instead of click steps (HELM1).

| HV1b | HV1 follow-up (the human, 2026-10-08): the client mixin `MixinSectionCompiler` failed injection in the dev client, so no world opened | done | Merged (`7c2da92d`). **Root cause:** Mixin resolves a bare `method = "compile"` to the first method of that name (`Quantifier.SINGLE`, `TargetSelectors.findRootTargets`); NeoForge's `SectionCompiler` has two: the 4-argument vanilla signature, patched into a forwarder with no `getBlockState` call, and the 5-argument overload (with `List<AddSectionGeometryEvent.AdditionalSectionRenderer>`) that holds the block loop and the only `RenderChunkRegion.getBlockState` call (javap on `neoforge-21.1.228-minecraft-merged.jar`). "Scanned 0 target(s)" is misleading (never incremented in Mixin 0.8.7). Fix: `method = {COMPILE_VANILLA, COMPILE_NEOFORGE}` with both full descriptors and `require = 1` (vanilla matches only the first, NeoForge wraps the one call in the second; no NeoForge type in the descriptor, so one shared mixin). **Headless check:** `neoforge/src/test/.../mixin/ClientMixinTargetsTest` (JUnit 5 + `asm-tree` 9.8 in `neoforge/build.gradle`, with `addModdingDependenciesTo(sourceSets.test)` putting the NeoForge-patched merged jar on the test classpath): asserts the patched class is the one read, resolves every injector's `method` selectors of every mixin in `pirates_n_ships.mixins.json` the way Mixin does and requires the `@At(INVOKE)` call sites (3 injectors checked, none skipped), and pins the water-plant wrap to one call in the 5-argument method; proven red on the old selector. `CLAUDE.md` Conventions: client mixins are never loaded by the GameTest server, the ASM test checks them, and "never loaded in a client" in a report is a blocker. 1742 + 4 JUnit, 646 GameTests ×1. Open: the test models root selection only (no `ordinal`, `slice`, regex or `@Desc`); Fabric needs a refmap and its own test at the port; Sable may also hook the compiler (in-game check). The human: open a world, then a hull over seagrass. |
| GR5 | Rope hauling (the human, 2026-10-08): sneak freezes the grapple rope's length, backing away pulls the hooked ship toward the player; plus the floating-rope bug | done | Merged (`5adea4d6`). `combat/grapple/GrappleHaul` (+ pure `HaulMath`): freeze when `grapple.hauling`, the hook is latched on a ship, the near end is in the thrower's hand (not tied to a cleat or ring), the thrower sneaks (`isShiftKeyDown`, no payload), is not riding and not standing on the hooked ship; frozen length = hand (eye − 0.45) to hook at sneak start. Tension per physics substep: `T = clamp(k·excess + c·extensionRate, 0, cap)` in force group `pirates_n_ships:haul` ("Hauling by Hand", `ShipBody.applyHaulImpulse`, horizontal at the hook's x/z at centre-of-mass height like the rope, sable-notes §9.0d; a resting ship woken once per tick); `haul_stiffness` 60 kpg/s², `haul_damping` 30 kpg/s, `haul_max_force` 200 kpg·m/s², `haul_player_pull` 0.08 blocks/tick. Slip: the frozen length grows to `d − cap/k` so the pull holds at the cap. The player feels nothing on the ground or a deck; airborne or swimming the rope cancels the outward velocity and pulls toward the hook. Hooks cannot attach to entities, so no entity hauling. The synced `TAUT` flag is also set while a frozen rope has excess. **No floating near end:** the mid-air pin is removed entirely (`RopeSlideService.pin`, `pinNearEnd`, their synced data and client cases); `nearEndFixed()` is true only for a cleat or ring tie; `tryBoard` on an untied rope gives the thrower a pull (`RopeRiderEntity.createPull`, feet straight to the landing spot at `slide.slide_min_speed`; let go where world blocks are in the way), others `Board.NOT_FIXED`; sliding needs a tied near end and a rider is dropped when the tie is lost; `ClientRopes.pick` skips other people's hand-held ropes. **Deviation:** with `hauling` on, a hand-held rope from land no longer drags the ship by itself (the G11 shore drag of 30 pulled constantly and broke "the rope pays out and the sloop stays"); a cleat or ring on land still drags; `hauling = false` restores it. 9 JUnit, 7 GameTests (`GrappleHaulGameTests`, own batches: haul ≥ 1 block in 5 s with peak tension ≤ cap, no sneak < 0.2 blocks, slip at cap 40, cleat ignores sneak, hauling off, airborne pull, the hand-held pull lands on the deck with no floating end and `NOT_FIXED` for others, haul and pull do not interfere), `GrappleSlideGameTests` reworked (slides tie to a cleat first), 602 GameTests ×2. Open: the pull's blocked check uses `Level#noCollision`, which does not see ship blocks; vanilla clears the sneak key on a sneak dismount; forces tuned on a 50 kpg hull, so a real sloop may need a higher cap; GR4's playtest steps 3, 7 and 9 describe the old pin (superseded by the GR5 section). Playtest `grapple.md` "GR5". |
| HV1 | Dry hull view (the human, 2026-10-07): water plants visible inside the hull; the underwater overlay while passing a deck trapdoor | done | Merged (`3fb92cbe`). **Overlay cause (proven by the new test before the fix):** `ship/hull/PartialCellRule` refused any partial cell with an open face to outside air, and the air above a deck hatch is outside air, so the hatch cell was never in the region while the hold cell below was; Sable's camera and eye checks test the exact point. Fix: outside air blocks a partial cell only while that outside cell is under the sea by more than 0.25 blocks above its lowest corner (sea level along the hull's up vector); `HullRuntime` counts the sea-blocked cells each tick and rebuilds the regions when the count changes. Kept limits: cells facing the sea stay out (no dry holes in the sea from outer slabs and stairs) and a partial cell must touch a dry compartment. **Plants:** block tag `pirates_n_ships:hidden_in_dry_hull` (seagrass, tall seagrass, kelp, kelp plant, sea pickle, bubble column) and the client mixin `MixinSectionCompiler` (`@WrapOperation` on `RenderChunkRegion#getBlockState` inside `SectionCompiler#compile`: a tagged block inside a dry region returns its bare fluid; no loader event can remove a block from a section, Sable only masks water, Sable's own compiler mixin injects elsewhere). `ship/hull/client/HiddenWaterPlants` + `RegionFootprint`: every `dry_hull_view.hidden_plants_refresh_ticks` (5) the client computes the regions' world footprint from Sable's `logicalPose`, publishes it immutably for the chunk threads, and re-marks only sections that `maybeHas` the tag and hold a tagged block at a changed position; measured worst case (a kelp forest, a 12×3×5 hold at 8 blocks/s) 7–10 sections per second, ~0.3 ms per refresh; a debug log every 200 ticks. Client config section `dry_hull_view` (`hide_water_plants`, `hidden_plants_refresh_ticks`; a client section cannot share the server's `dry_hull` name). 10 JUnit, 3 GameTests (`DryHullViewGameTests`), one existing count 25 → 26, 1643 JUnit, 597 GameTests ×2. Open: **the client mixin has never loaded in a client** (the GameTest server is dedicated; a mismatch would crash the first chunk build: the human's first `runClient` is the check); Sodium or Embeddium replace the compiler; the hidden set lags the ship by up to one refresh; a hatch's outside air bobbing at the 0.25 margin can flip the cell. Playtest `dry-hull.md`. |
| SH1 | Sail heel (the human, 2026-10-07: "a little thrust tips the boat extremely"): measure the starter sloop's heel, add a righting torque model and a heel cap | done | Merged (`f2cafa1d`, with SH1b). **Measured before** (starter sloop, 320 kpg, beam wind, 10 s then furled): full sail 8.5° at 6 blocks/s and 14.2° at 12, 73° and capsized at the strongest wind (37); it never got back under 2° after furling. **Causes:** (1) no righting moment beyond Sable's block buoyancy (effective GM ≈ 1.1 blocks at 1° falling to 0.65 at 9°, collapsing past ~20°); (2) **the starter sloop template has a 3-block hole in its stern bottom** (template y 1, z 25, x 3..5), so the hold is open to the sea, the ship floats a block too deep and flooding pockets hold a list (fixed in SH1b); (3) the sail's centre of effort 8.7 blocks above the centre of mass plus the keel's lateral force 2.3 below (+22 %); (4) the dry lift at a fixed centroid between re-analyses (−2700 N·blocks/rad on the sealed sloop; follow-up: apply it at its metacentre). Pure `sailing/force/RightingModel` applied in `SailingRuntime.physicsTick` to every afloat ship: GM = clamp(B² / (12·max(T, 0.5)) × `righting_factor`, 0, `max_metacentric_height` 1.5), K = m·g·GM, τ = −s·(K·clamp(φ, ±`max_righting_degrees` 30) + 2ζ√(K·I)·ω) with ζ = `righting_damping` 0.4 and the submerged fraction s; pitch off by default (`pitch_righting_factor` 0: Sable holds the trim and it broke the cargo, kedge and mob tests on small hulls); the sail, keel, rudder and anchor moments' roll and pitch are scaled by `sail_heel_factor` then capped at `max_heel_torque_per_mass` 3 × m and faded over the last 10° before `max_heel_degrees` 25 (never the righting direction); a `righting` line in `/pirates ship forces`; config `stability.*` with `enabled`. **After:** full 6 → 2.3°, full 12 → 4.6°, strongest → 10.0° with no capsize, back under 2° in 0.5–1.4 s (4 s at the strongest); the 7×17 hull 0.3–0.6°; `stability` off reproduces 14°. Side effects: beam reach faster with less leeway (`LEEWAY_RATIO` 0.37 → 0.2), WV1's storm roll of the 7×17 hull 4.35° → 1.85°, the CW1 chest trim −1.70 → −1.22, the whirlpool test's small hull no longer rolls over (thresholds updated in SH1b). 11 JUnit, 11 GameTests in own batches, 656 GameTests ×3 (two foreign thresholds red until SH1b). Open: the dry lift's fixed centroid; no flood-based reduction of the righting torque (a flooded ship stays upright); GM from the plot box overstates wide rigs (bounded by the cap); WV1 may retune its storm forcing for small hulls. Playtest `milestone-3.md` 5d. |
| SH1b | SH1 follow-up: the two measured thresholds and the starter sloop template's open stern bottom | done | Merged (`f2cafa1d`, with SH1). **Template:** the starter sloop comes from the generator `art/schematics/starter_sloop.py`; its bottom layer stopped one block short of the layer above at the stern, leaving the hold cells (3..5, 2, 25) without a floor; the generator now lays a bottom plank under every hollow hold cell of layer 2 that has none (3 dark oak planks, 680 → 683 blocks), rebuilt through the lab (`build_structures.Lab`, documented in `art/README.md` "Generated templates": edit the generator, never the `.schem`) and reconverted; size, helm, mast, berth and stations unchanged. **Measured after:** strongest wind at full sail then furled: steady heel 7.5–9.4°, max ≤ 9.5°, back under 2° in 1.65–2.1 s, 0.4–1.6° left after 5 s, so `sloopFullStrongest` has its 2° rule back. Thresholds: the whirlpool's small hull now moves 0.44–0.46 blocks (assert > 0.15) and the 7×17 hull's storm roll is 1.85° (assert 1..15); `heavyShipMovesLessThanALightOne` also broke since SH1 (the light hull moves 0.20–0.42 instead of 0.66–0.83 and the orbit swamps the pull): the light bound is 0.13 and "moves less" is now the horizontal distance travelled < 0.8 × the light one. 725 GameTests ×3 (13 runs in all, earlier failures only on that test with its old rules). Risk: the heavy/light distance ratio measured 0.77–0.78 against the 0.8 bound, so that test may flake (a longer window or a look at the righting torque's effect on the ballasted hull's drift); the sloop rests with 4.2° of pitch; `CargoWeight.java:56` still says 680 blocks. |
| AN2a | Anchor physics (the human, 2026-10-07): a free-falling anchor with offset, a chain force that decelerates and swings the ship instead of stopping it, dragging past the holding power | done | Merged (`370d83d1`). `sailing/anchor`: pure `AnchorMotion` (the drop starts at the hawse with its velocity; 20 blocks/s² in air, in water the vertical speed settles to `sink_speed` 4 and the horizontal speed decays at `water_drag` 2/s; lands on the first solid block; the paid-out length grows with the hawse distance up to `sailing_runtime.anchor_chain_length` 32 and **freezes on landing** (the capstan's brake), else a ship would run out all 32 blocks before the chain came taut; the anchor is a world entity placed from the state every tick, never a rider), pure `AnchorChain` + `AnchorPhysics.onPhysicsTick` (only while resting and taut: pull = m·(`chain_stiffness` 5 × stretch + `chain_damping` 2 × separating speed), never pushing, plus `settle_drag` 0.2; horizontal part only, capped at m·`holding_force` 10; roll and pitch scaled by `heel_factor` 0.25, yaw full; force group `pirates_n_ships:anchor`; 1 kpg of Sable mass = 1 ton), dragging (when the spring would pass the cap the anchor slides toward the hawse at `drag_scrape_rate` 1 block/s, climbs one-block steps, is caught by a wall; past 1 block of slack it follows the ship), raising (`raise_speed` 2.5: slides over the seabed until the chain reaches it, breaks out and rises within 0.5 blocks horizontally; stowed at length 0; the capstan mid-way lets go again), `SailingRuntime.isAnchored()` (resting, holding, no drag in 10 ticks, slower than `at_rest_speed` 0.3; otherwise anchor down with way on; `/pirates ship forces` shows the chain), `AnchorStatus` synced for AN2b (paid-out length, resting/taut/dragging/holding/anchored flags, the world hawse relative to the anchor). The old stop (`AnchorModel`, `ShipForceModel.Anchor`, `SailingParams.AnchorParams`, six `sailing.anchor_*` values, the timed hold ramp) is removed; pre-AN2a saves load. **Finding:** Sable's water drag alone stops a coasting hull in about 2 s (decay ≈ 1.6/s) and the keel's yaw damping caps a momentum-only turn at ~5°, so the hard turn happens with the sails drawing (test (a) uses a world-fixed drive of 8 blocks/s² per ton). Measured on a 9×17 hull over a 6-deep seabed: drop at 4.9 blocks/s, landing after 29 ticks 3.7 blocks astern of the hawse (3.4 ahead of the release point in the world), yaw 36° to starboard within 5 s of taut and 59° after 10 s, worst single-tick speed loss 7.3 %, way along the heading 7–12 % after 10 s; `holding_force` 3 drags 8.3 blocks; `holding_force` 0 never holds; under sail the chain stretches ≤ 0.30 blocks over 20 s and raising takes 48 ticks. New JUnit `AnchorMotionTest`, `AnchorChainTest`, `AnchorRulesTest` rewritten; 4 GameTests in own batches, the control tests adapted; 650 GameTests ×3. Open: no extra scope, so a ship under weak sail sits nearly above its anchor; a sleeping body under a steady chain force untested; defaults tuned to the test drive, in-game sails are weaker so turns will be gentler. Playtest `anchor.md`. |

| GR6 | GameTests run on a pinned calm sea: `hookOnAWorldBlockFromAShipHaulsTheShipTowardIt` stalled at rope 2.05 after WAV2 (a clear-weather test world is calm or moderate by a game-time noise; moderate now carries 5× the torque and heave) | done | Merged (`9348d8c4`). The premise was wrong: since WV1 `SeaStates.stillByDefault` makes every GameTest level's field `WaveField.FLAT` unless a test holds a state, so no wave torque or heave ever acted on the grapple test; only the reported state varied. The failure was a timeout race: a hull that does not yaw hauls straight at 0.27 blocks/s, reaches the hold length at ~tick 1040 and creeps its last 0.4 blocks at 0.04–0.05 blocks/s; far from the origin f32 rounding freezes the creep and `GrappleRules.Holding` declares the stall (pass), near the origin (x 440, 873) the creep stays above the detector's 0.01 blocks per 20 substeps and the rope is still taut at tick 1200 (1 of 196 flat copies, 2 of 25 moderate). Fix: `SeaStateModel.weatherTarget(gameTestServer, …)` reports CALM on a `GameTestServer` whatever the weather (a hold still wins; `SeaStates.clear` drops the level's entry so the next test starts calm and flat), and the kedge test's timeout is 1800 ticks with the measurements in its comment. 2 JUnit, 1 GameTest (`aPlainTestLevelHasACalmFlatSea`, also proving the Fabric runner is a `GameTestServer`); grapple/wave/settings 31 scoped ×3 NeoForge, Fabric 31. Open: the 1800 rests on an extrapolated slope; a real kedge rope can stay taut a few extra seconds near the origin (gameplay, harmless). |

| WS4a-b | WS4a follow-up: `GunneryGameTests.atWillHitsMostShots` hit 2 of 5 in the merge stage | done | Merged (`ea1101a5`, with WS4a). **A real bug:** 40 logged shots left the same point at the same velocity every time (no spread, no barrel step between aim and shot, the simulated flight matched the real one); the only random part is `npc_block_damage_multiplier` 0.5, so once hits had broken the near and far wall blocks on the gun's single line, every later ball flew through the hull and `GunCrewAim` still called the path a hit because it only checked the bounds. Fix: `Target` carries a `solid` predicate (`Gunnery.solidAt` turns the world point into plot coordinates and tests the plot block's collision shape, the same thing a ball's collider hits), `firstSolid` samples the clipped path every 0.05 blocks, `solve` prefers steps that strike a solid block and otherwise holds fire; `aim_tolerance` removed. Tests: `atWillHitsEveryShot` (block damage 0, deterministic 5/5), `aBreachedLineMakesTheCrewTakeAnotherStep`, `withBlockDamageEveryShotFiredHits` (every shot fired hits, 7/7 and 3/3); 13 JUnit; 697 GameTests ×3. Open: a cannon cannot traverse, so against a still target it has about two lines and holds fire once both are tunnelled (a follow-up could vary the aim point or turn the gun). |

| HZ1 | The whirlpool pull test flakes (`heavyShipMovesLessThanALightOne`, SH1b's watch item) | done | Merged (`74cf7515`). **Cause: the test, not the whirlpool.** Sable's physics (Rapier) stores world positions as 32-bit floats and updates them ~720 times a second, so an axis speed below ~360 × the f32 step at that coordinate is rounded away and one just above is rounded up: the heavy hull's x stayed frozen for 110 ticks at x 2737 while reporting −0.069 m/s, drift at x 1616 was 1.27× that at x 202 (exactly one f32 step), and in one run the light hull's x froze at 4258 so the orbit carried it outward (the stage's "not pulled in: −0.36"). The GameTest grid lands anywhere within ±4096 blocks, so pose-based numbers swung from run to run. Ruled out: the pull force, mass, interference, heel, async re-analysis. Fix: the test integrates `ShipBody.linearVelocity()` × 0.05 s per tick (`Track`) over ticks 10–160 instead of comparing poses; bounds light radial > 0.08 (measured 0.17–0.32), heavy travel < 0.8 × light (now 0.56–0.59). 10 of 10 scoped runs. `docs/sable-notes.md` §9.0l: never compare poses for drifts below ~0.2 m/s on an axis, integrate velocity; §9.0e's rule of thumb understated the threshold ~18×. **Gameplay consequence worth reporting upstream:** 10,000 blocks from the origin a ship slower than ~0.35 m/s on an axis does not move along it, at 100,000 blocks ~2.8 m/s. Follow-up HZ1b: `smallShipIsPulledTowardTheCentre` has the same weakness beyond |x| 4096; other pose-based slow-drift tests may too. |
| CW1b | The cargo sinking test flakes (`chestOfIronSinksAndTrimsTheShip`, sink 0.304 < 0.4, three times under load) | done | Merged (`aa481b82`). **Cause: a Sable bug, phantom blocks on a reused plot.** Removing a sub-level frees its plot at once (`SubLevelContainer#removeSubLevel`) but `RapierPhysicsPipeline#remove` drops only the body; the plot's native chunk sections and their physics tickets stay until a ticket update ≥ 20 ticks later; a ship assembled into that plot in the same tick finds the old tickets, `addSectionIfNotTracked` skips the fresh upload and `changeBlock` writes only the new blocks into the old sections, so the removed ship's blocks stay as phantom voxels wherever the new ship has air: they float, drag and collide. In a big batch the first ship a test assembles often gets a plot another test freed that tick; the empty-chest hull trimmed 0.21–0.43 instead of 0.046 and the measured sink ranged 0.047–0.99. Reproduced deterministically (a ghost ship removed and a hull assembled into its plot the same tick trims −0.65); ruled out by measurement: late cargo mass, grounding (clearance ≥ 2.5), async analysis latency, f32 coordinates, reuse a tick later. Fix in `ship/sable/SableShips` (sable-common calls with file references): the removal observer drops every plot section of a leaving sub-level (`handleChunkSectionRemoval`, observers run before `onRemove`), `assemble` re-uploads and re-tickets every plot section of the new ship, and Sable-created sub-levels (splits, loads) within 40 ticks of a removal get the same refresh; either half alone is not enough. New regression test `AssemblyGameTests.plotFreedThisTickHasNoPhantomBlocks` (own batch; 0.24 blocks of difference without the fix, 0.0000 with). 10 × CargoWeight scoped green (sink 0.658–0.674), the reproducing 12-class set 5 × 152 green, 58 related tests green. Answers: Sable's physics is not paced by real time (exactly `substepsPerTick` substeps on the server thread); load only changes which tests overlap in a tick, so the kedge haul flake should be read the same way. Likely also the Q6 calm-basin flake (§9.0k). `docs/sable-notes.md` §9.0m; **report upstream with §9.0c.** Open: the workaround uploads ~24 sections per plot chunk at every assembly, unprofiled for very large ships. |
| PHY1 | Physics test hygiene: slow-drift tests by integrated velocity (HZ1's rule), the heel tests in a deeper basin (SH2's finding) | done | Merged (`3002cce4`). Shared `core/gametest/ShipTrack` (takes a `ShipBody`; `follow` ticks itself; `dx`, `dz`, `travel`, `radialMove`, pose and tilt for the log) replaces HZ1's private helper; converted: both whirlpool tests (`smallShipIsPulledTowardTheCentre` 0.415–0.454 in over ten runs at x −2810..3086; its comment wrongly said the hull stays upright, it ends ~35° tilted), the grapple "hull stays" checks (0.000), and the one-block grapple haul (102 ticks by velocity at ~0.2 m/s; timeout 130 → 160 with the measured reason, bound unchanged). | CRW2b | `DesertionGameTests.deserterLeavesAnywhereAfterWaiting` fails in full runs only (twice on the stage, same preceding batches) | done | Merged (`f1e43231`). **Cause: a race inside the test.** `Desertions.onLevelTick` runs its own look whenever `gameTime % 100 == 0`; the test marked the deserter at tick 0 and waited for the ship's bounds before its own `Desertions.run`, so when a multiple of 100 fell into the wait the level's look had already sent the member off and the test found nobody leaving. Full runs start batches at arbitrary game times and load widens the wait (the bounds wait made it worse). A second hazard: a batch that leaves the level at night makes the test's `morning()` a dawn that clears marks of members with fine morale. Fix (test only): the three waiting tests set their marks inside the `whenReady` body in the same tick as their own run; the port test registers its port there too. Reproduced deterministically with a forced 101-tick wait, then fixed under the same race; 5 × 47 combined scoped runs green. The preceding tests leave nothing behind and `Desertions` is right. Risk: other tests that set state and wait before calling `Desertions.run` or `ShipDayTick.dawn` themselves could race the 100-tick look or a dawn. |

| UPK1 | The upkeep GameTests survive a preceding night: `walletPaysWhatTheChestCannot` failed once in a full Fabric run (morale 36, a stray dawn) | done | Merged (`81eb930d`). The night hypothesis was wrong: the loaders' **config file watcher** (FML on NeoForge, Forge Config API Port on Fabric) saw the server config the test run had just created, reloaded it from disk 2–3 s later on its own thread, and silently undid every `ConfigOverrides` value active at that moment (36 = 70 − 35 hungry and thirsty + 1, i.e. the payday with provisions back on). Both GameTest tasks now write `disableConfigWatcher = true` into `build/gametest/config/fml.toml` / `forgeconfigapiport.toml` before each run (0 reloads logged in 5 NeoForge and 3 Fabric runs, 1–2 before). Second hazard kept as defence: `morning()` in `UpkeepGameTests` and `DesertionGameTests` calls `ShipDayTick.observe` right after setting the clock, `MealGameTests.ship()` sets a morning before the crew exists (with night forced and no guard, 7 of 10 upkeep tests failed). No gameplay change. Open: an IDE run keeps the watcher on; `ConfigOverrides` could re-apply itself on a config reload through a platform hook. |

Measured: 7×17 hull storm ±4.3°, calm 0.36°, settles in 10 s; a 32×12 hull 0.7°; a side hatch 0.4 above the still sea took 1.5–3.2 blocks of water in 300 storm ticks, none in calm. Config `waves.*` (`enabled`, `amplitude`, `ship_torque`, `max_torque_per_mass`, `state_change_seconds`, `spill`, `sync_interval_ticks`), client `wave_effects.camera_sway` (now false), `camera_sway_fraction` 0.5, `spray`; kept in `hazard/HazardConfig`. **The GameTest server keeps a flat sea unless a test holds one** (`SeaStates.stillByDefault`): the test world's clear weather otherwise tilted the basin hull 7.8° and failed `shipAtRestStaysAtRest`. 4 JUnit classes, 8 GameTests (own batches), 592 GameTests ×2. | WV1b | WV1 follow-up: `WaveGameTests.stormSpillsThroughALowHatch` depended on the wave phase (0.17 blocks in the merge stage against 1.5–3.2 measured by the agent) | done | Merged (`a10e67f8`, with WV1). **Proven cause:** the field's phase is `k·r·anchor − ω·gameTime`, so it depended on the game time the test started at and where the runner placed the structure; and WV1's hatch sat in the west wall 2 blocks off the roll axis, where the roll (phase-locked to the crests) swung the sill between −1.6 and +1.4 blocks: in some phases the roll lifted the hatch with every crest (0.17 blocks in 300 ticks), in others it put the hatch under the still sea and the hull flooded fully (18). Fix: a test-only `WaveField.Origin(ticks, x, z)` held by the tracker's override (`SeaStates.set(level, state, dir, until, origin)`; gameplay uses `Origin.NONE`), `hold()` pins the origin to the test's start time and structure centre, the spill hatch moved to the middle of the south wall on the roll axis, the window is one beat (480 ticks), and the threshold follows from the orifice law with the hull held still (`E = Σ c·min(1, e)·√e`, water ≥ min(E, 18)/2, E ≥ 1, 0 < sill < 1.2). The roll tests are pinned too and now repeat exactly (storm 4.35°, calm 0.36°, big hull 0.69°). 2 JUnit, 602 GameTests ×3 plus one run at the slowest phase. Risk noted: `HullRuntime.seaShipFrame` sometimes reports no sea for a tick on a hull rolling in a storm (freeboard −2321939 in the log); and a small hull's open side hatch floods fully within seconds in a storm. |

| Q6 | Flaky physics GameTest under load: `DryHullGameTests.hullAfloatInBasinStaysCalm` failed four times on 2026-10-07/08 (tilt 9.17° and 9.15°, drift 1.063 and 1.050 m/s) in stages and agent runs with six to nine GameTest servers running, and passed in about forty other rounds | done | Merged (`790c429c`). **Not reproduced:** about 30 full-suite runs alone and under load (up to six servers at once, load average to 265, one run taking 13.6 min) gave the identical time series every time (1.8 m/s at tick 10, 0.657 at tick 40, at rest from tick 140, tilt ≤ 3e-5°); the three failing positions replayed identically; no other ship or entity ever entered the test area; no force of ours breaks the symmetry (no container for CW1, damping and keel act on motion only, the dry-volume lift acts at the symmetric centroid, no re-analysis). **Sable's physics is not wall-clock dependent:** `SubLevelPhysicsSystem#tickPipelinePhysics` runs a fixed `substepsPerTick` (2) of exactly 1/40 s per server tick on the server thread, and the GameTest server never sleeps between ticks; Rapier's parallel feature spreads only the 14th digit (`docs/sable-notes.md` §9.0k). A clue that fits the two 1.05 m/s drifts: the hull is very sensitive to the water under it (two dry columns for 30 ticks → 53°; a bob starting 20 ticks late → about 1.03 m/s at tick 40), but no cause for a late refill was found. Mitigation: the test runs in its own batch `pirates_n_ships_dry_hull_calm` (49 tests no longer run beside it), keeps its limits, adds a rest check from tick 140 (< 0.05 m/s, < 0.5°), and on failure names the tick of each maximum, every ship or entity within 2 blocks of the area and a 20-tick trace (`othersIn`). 594 GameTests ×3. Open: the cause; if another physics test flakes in a shared batch, add the same diagnostics there. |

The agent's Gradle runs were blocked by the classifier; the merge stage compiled and verified it with four GameTest rounds. |

| GR4 | Grapple follow-ups from the human's playtest (2026-10-07): musket-only launching, the accidental rope grab after a shot, reach 32 by hand and 64 from the musket, and the hook sticking to any surface | done | Merged (`f2b5343e`). **Cause of the grab:** `GrappleClient.onInteraction` ran the rope pick on every main-hand use before the item's own use, and a thrower's untied rope starts at their hand, so looking along a fresh shot picked it (0.45 blocks from the ray, inside `board_pick_radius`), the server's `tryBoard` accepted it and pinned the near end where the player stood. Fix: pure `RopeSlide.grab` (an empty main hand or a hook in it, and `hook.tickCount ≥ grapple.slide.grab_cooldown_ticks` 20) checked on the server and mirrored on the client, so a firearm's use always wins. Removed: `CrossbowHookLaunch`, `FireLoadedHookPayload`, the crossbow branches, `crossbow_*` config and tests; a hook next to a crossbow is vanilla (with arrows the crossbow draws, without, vanilla hands the use to the off hand and the hook is thrown). Reach, measured by two GameTests (a level flight from eye height): `max_rope_length` 32, `throw_velocity` 3.0, `gravity` 0.02 (a free throw lands at 39.4 blocks); `musket_rope_length` 64, `musket_speed` 1.6 (× 3.0), new `musket_gravity_factor` 0.5 synced to the client (83.4 blocks). **Any surface:** pure `GrappleRules.blockHit` (LATCH / LATCH_OWN_SHIP / LATCH_WORLD / OWN_SHIP / LAND / SLIP) and `haul` (SHIPS / SHORE / KEDGE / NONE); a world latch is `shipId == null` with world coordinates, released when the block breaks; the kedge pulls the near end's ship with `haul_force` through the same spring; toggles `grapple.latch_world_blocks`, `latch_own_ship`; tag `#pirates_n_ships:grapple_slips` (leaves, glass panes). 66 grapple JUnit, GameTests for the re-aim, own ship, kedge, zip line, slipping, reach and the crossbow; 530 GameTests ×2. Open: the kedge is slow (0.34 m/s, a separate kedge force may be wanted); a 3.0 throw is near a full bow shot; hooks latch on barriers and untagged collision shapes; the client's cooldown mirror uses the client tick count. Playtest `grapple.md` "GR4". |
| HL1 | Helm replacement (the human, 2026-10-07: a destroyed and re-placed helm left the anchor and sail block saying "must be on an assembled ship") | done | Merged (`729acb57`). **Root cause found:** breaking the helm on a healthy ship breaks nothing (the body, its UUID, the record and the stations stay; losing the heat-map root only rebuilds the heat map). The failure needs an earlier split: after a split that cut every block off the root, Sable's `solidCount` is reset to the largest group and then lowered again as the other groups move out, so the count is too low; at the next root loss the remaining blocks match neither "whole" nor "all pieces" and Sable moves every block into a **new sub-level**, and `SubLevelContainer#tick` removes the emptied parent before the observers deliver the split; our removal listener deleted the record, so the new body had no record and no pointer and its winch and capstan refused (reproduced deterministically at Sable's root in `theShipFollowsTheBodySableMovesItTo`). Fix: `SableSplits` saves the parent's user data at announce time and delivers every announced parent; the record is held back while a split is pending (`isSplitPending`) and the identity, name, origin and steering helm move to the keeper; `ShipSplits.carryOnIdentityMove` copies position-free user data. Helm handling: the steering helm's plot position lives in the ship pointer and is checked lazily (`ShipHelm`, pure `HelmRules`); the first helm placed or used on a helmless ship becomes the steering helm, a second helm neither steers nor disassembles while the first stands; a helmless ship keeps its bow; a helm used on a Sable body without our pointer **adopts** it as a new ship (owner = the player, `assembly.enabled` applies), which recovers the human's orphaned ship. `SableShips.heatMapRoot`. 5 JUnit, 6 GameTests, 529 GameTests ×2 in its worktree; in the merge stage `aSecondHelmWaitsUntilTheFirstIsGone` failed once in three runs ("the sailing runtime has no helm"), the order-dependent two-helm runtime bug HL1b fixes, so HL1 landed together with HL1b. Open: a real Sable save and load of a helmless ship is only simulated; other modules see the moved ship's old body as destroyed and drop their per-ship state (stations, flood state, anchor); the Sable count bug itself remains. Follow-up HL1b: `SailingRuntimes.onBlockChanged` must prefer `ShipHelm.steering` (two helms: breaking the second turned off the first's rudder) and the sailing module must carry the bow on an identity move. Playtest `milestone-1.md` "HL1". |
| FL2 | Flags in the world (§4.7, audit gap): allegiance from the flagpoles, navy and pirate reactions, false colours detection, struck-colours and neutral-ship crimes | done | Merged (`000bbea`). `ShipData.flag` is a `FlagReading` (saved `""` / `"jolly_roger"` / `"struck:navy"`, old strings load as no flag) plus `blownCoverUntil`; `ship/decor/flag/ShipAllegiance` refreshes it on every pole event (`FlagpoleEvents.fire` first), on assembly and for the keeper after a split or rejoin, finding poles through the plot chunks' block entities (`ServerChunkCacheMixin#getChunkNow`); wrecks fly nothing. Pure `law/flag/ShipStance` (NONE / JOLLY_ROGER / UNMASKED / SURRENDERED; surrender beats a blown cover beats the Jolly Roger) feeds `mob/HostilityRules.Target.ship`: nobody attacks a surrendered crew on sight, the navy attacks aboard a Jolly Roger or unmasked ship, pirates leave a Jolly Roger ship alone. `law/world/FlagCrimes`: every `observe_interval_ticks` navy mobs within `observe_range` of a Jolly Roger ship charge `seen_under_jolly_roger` (once per repeat window); for a navy flag the interim rule `FlagLaw.fliesFalseColours` (standing 0 for all, owner wanted level ≥ `false_flag_wanted_threshold` 1) lets each observer roll `FalseColorsDetection`; a detection records `caught_false_colors`, blows the cover for `blown_cover_ticks` 6000 and tells the owner. `combat/cannon/CannonShipHits` listener (balls remember their firing ship): a hit on a struck ship → `attack_struck_colors`, on a merchant or banner flag → `attack_neutral_ship`, never on one's own ship; the offender is the shooting player or the firing ship's online owner. Config `law.flags.*`. 4 JUnit classes, 10 GameTests (each in its own batch; 525 GameTests ×2 after merging main). Open: observation and hit crimes need the owner online; detection at defaults takes ~17 s within 16 blocks for a suspect (playtest judges); the Jolly Roger charge spams "repeat ignored" log entries every interval; NPC cannons have no autonomous targeting, so surrender acts on duelists and musketeers. Playtest `flags.md` "FL2". |

Group `navy_outpost`, seven pieces: `fort_gate` 15×10×15 (start; sea gate north, parade court with a navy flag on a five-block pole, the harbor desk and notice board in an office, crates and barrels; `quay_out` N, `wall_east_out` E, `wall_west_out` W, `building_out` S on the landward apron), `quay` 7×9×18 (stone quay, masonry five down, rings, cleats, bollards, crane arm, two berths at y 4), `wall` 7×8×7 (curtain wall with walkway and merlons, a two-block cannon facing seaward with a powder barrel, a ladder; carries all four wall jigsaws), `wall_tower` 7×12×7 (terminator for either run, flag), `barracks` 11×9×9, `brig` 9×8×9 (two cells of brig bars and doors, unlocked), `watchtower` 5×14×5. **Decision:** jigsaw rotates but never mirrors, so each wall run has its own pair (`wall_east_in/out` row z 2, `wall_west_in/out` row z 3); the unused pair on a placed wall points back into its parent, finds no room and becomes stone bricks. Pools `navy_outpost/start|walls|buildings|quay|terminators`. `StructurePiecesTest` per group with navy checks (rows line up, the cannon's halves and clear muzzle, flags, brig doors, desk and board, berths). 495 GameTests. Open: chests and barrels are empty (no loot tables), the cannon is unloaded (a garrison needs powder and shot), `_style.flagpole` writes the flag block entity through the jigsaw list (a `buildspec.py` method would be cleaner). `art/README.md` "Navy outpost (ST3)". |

Changes: `SailingRuntimes.refreshSails` re-reads yards and cleats from the plot before the winch answers and logs WARN "Sailing state of ship … was stale"; pure `station/winch/YardDiagnosis` (HEADS_SAIL, NOTHING_BELOW, BLOCKED, TOO_CLOSE, OTHER_AXIS, OFF_MIDDLE, LOWER_TOO_LONG); `RiggingReport` + `/pirates ship rigging` (sails, per-yard reason, triangular sails, sailing state, crew aboard); `station/OrderHints.why` replaces a bare "0 of 0" with no ship under you / no station can … / no sails: <reason> / nothing to do / nobody to …; the crew's answer names the reason. "0 of 0" before CR1 was the expected line for unassigned crew. 4 JUnit, 11 GameTests (each in its own batch), 534 GameTests ×2. **Needs the human:** `/pirates ship rigging` on the original ship before changing anything, plus `latest.log` (look for "was stale"). Open: `refreshSails` scans the plot per winch order while a ship has no sails. Playtest `crew-npc.md` "Q5", `milestone-3.md` §6b step 7. |
